"Stopped → command acceleration" 0.77–0.997. Command cost under-states realized cost by about 10 % (e.g. 77 581 vs 85 948). Under RL-H_0, L-ACT_0 commands nv < 0.1 on 23–24 % of decisions (vs 0.000 under ACT): it brakes to a stop when the light leaves its support. | **Implementation-vs-intent mismatch, confirmed** (the declared intent is a physical idle penalty). The IDLE term is functionally inert for these fleets, and the learned behaviour is "press the accelerator whenever stopped". All approach-cost numbers (Q7, Q7b, C-12's r) are COMMAND costs: keep them, but label them. Physical conclusions must use tripinfo time loss, realized idle and fuel. The 23 % commanded stops under RL-H are direct evidence for the phantom-braking mechanism (B4F). Correction for new training: `CarRL` v2 with realized idle (cost charged one step later on the realized speed), versioned, plus a sensitivity retraining; old checkpoints are not reinterpreted. |

Repairing its REACTION (release to the human driver model outside its support) removes about 70 % of the city-level harm with no retraining and no messages, and is exactly a no-op in the training environment. Prior art: Simplex / OOD gatekeepers and compositional shielding (2606.14130); the gate is a baseline-class mechanism. What is new is the measured asymmetry (input ✗, reaction ✓) and the identity guarantee. |

Within every family except SIM-halt the fleet dominates (0.62–0.98). **In own pairs, agents lose MORE time than humans in every family:** S1 30.6 vs 24.2; SIM-halt 35.6 vs 27.2; H 56.5 vs 33.9. | Co-training as implemented (independent DQN learners, 120 episodes) neither improves the matched pair nor creates partner specificity. It mainly produces fragile fleets. The B3 hypotheses are closed as negative. The deeper finding: the learned fleets are not physical upgrades over human drivers (agent TL > human TL), consistent with C-13 (their reward's idle term is avoided by command). Any "fleet upgrade" study must therefore define the upgrade physically (F-branch U-1) and should use reward-v2 fleets. The interaction share 0.52 over the full matrix comes from fragile fleets meeting some lights. It is the city analogue of "standalone score hides partner dependence" (review R2 §1). |

D1 rows on TRAIN 0–7: always-SUMO 0 and σ 0.3 0 teleports on every seed, but GLOSA 0–2, IC-GLOSA 0–10, R5h_t0 2–6, R5h_t1 2–17, PPO snapshots 4–14 and BC 5–14 per run, mostly even counts. It teleports together with either the agent abreast that needs the opposite swap (GLOSA: lockstep under the identical command v = d / ttg) or the car queued behind it (PPO / IC). `setSpeed` overrides SUMO's strategic and cooperative speed adaptation for lane changes, both for the changer and for commanded cars on the target lane. **Consequence for the record:** every learned-fleet TL / human-TL / teleport result so far (C-21…C-31, C-39…C-42, and the B / v9 / v10 pilots in progress) includes a deadlock cost created by the actuation interface rather than by the policy's eco-driving. Bookkeeping is exact: the logged per-second costs summed per decision equal the stored rewards (max difference 0). The convention is: drop the incomplete last transition, and bootstrap the previous decision at the popped decision's observation (the observed boundary state). Its magnitude is negligible here, but it is named and not described as "all post-release cost is learned". **(B) Discount unit:** **97.9 %** of consecutive decisions in a segment are 1 s apart (p99 14–19 s, max 90–99, 1.1–1.2 % above 10 s); a decision carries 1.7 credited seconds on average (max 99–101). `V8.gae` discounts once per DECISION, with the in-between seconds undiscounted, so R10 optimises an **intervention-indexed objective**. The per-segment return with γ per decision is median **1.12–1.13×** the return with γ per second (p10 1.02, p90 1.41), i.e. a somewhat longer horizon in seconds. This is declared as R10's objective. It is not the per-second objective of v8, and the naive-GAE identity in K10-2 checks only the recursion, not this choice. **(C) Training trend, read per trip:** per traffic seed (latest − first visit, mean over 8), decisions per episode change by −0.1…+0.1 % and the cost per trip (segment return) falls by **3.2–4.0 %** on all six seeds, with 0 teleports throughout. | C-63 (17:17, 04/10) | **Corrections from the M3 audit** (`…/catchup/M3_E_VS_COMMITMENT_AND_ORACLE_AUDIT_20261004.md`). (1) **C-61 is NOT an upper bound** ("trần" withdrawn). It gives exact future switches of ONE replayed schedule to two frozen policies; it does not optimise over messages, policies or schedules, and for a fixed sub-optimal receiver the true value is not necessarily the best message. The replay is open-loop: 5–7 teleports in 20 runs, human TL +0.55. **Allowed reading:** on these schedules an eco-driving opportunity still exists for the frozen fleets. It is NOT established that the remaining problem is information only, nor that any deployable forecast or commitment reaches the oracle level in closed loop. (2) **Information-only methods (forecast C / D) do not cost "zero":** they need data, compute and communication, and the fleet's changed behaviour changes queues and therefore the frozen light's actions. Write "no light weights updated and no light action constrained", and measure the closed-loop cost. (3) **Novelty corrections.** D, a policy-based SPaT forecast, has a direct precedent (HONEST-CAV §III-B: the light policy on the current state, blended with phase history). Training against a frozen partner is not new in itself (Fictitious Co-Play §2.1). PCT (CVPR 2021 §4) is inspiration only: in traffic, a changed action or a wrong forecast is not equivalent to harm, so regression must be defined physically. **E has potential; its novelty is NOT established.** (4) **B is kept as a cheap comparator.** Its closeness to priority requests limits the novelty claim but does not show that B loses. (5) The forecast is a narrow baseline: phase-duration history only, no queue or policy input, and validation was not at the fleet's actual query times. | audit file |

| C-65 (17:57, 04/10) | **M3 protocol Amendment A1** (hash after A1: 15c07afd9307089b; responds to the pre-screen review of d1d76226). Changes:
- **B1:** a common PHYSICAL base reward (realised delay share + 0.5·fuel g/s, over each light's decision interval, all vehicles), with E0 = λ 0.
- **B2 / B3:** event tickets, issued at every decision tick with weights fixed at issue. The penalty is charged to the CURRENT action using open tickets aged 5–20 s, as integrated late or early seconds, with a pending-transition credit fix.
- **B4:** weights normalised to mean 1 on one common pre-fine-tune TRAIN trace, clipped at 10. A new **E-active** (count on the receiver's eligible set) is added. All E arms see the same 5 zero-initialised extra inputs (ticket state and weight sums).
- **B5:** receiver sensitivity is computed with a pure probe that perturbs ttg in BOTH the rule and the residual observation.
- **B6:** a 3-block provider lineage (refit after blocks 1 and 2, block 3 trains with the deployed provider). The "calibrate on DEV" step is withdrawn.
- **C1:** the gate is superiority on agent fuel vs R, E0, E-uniform, E-ETA, E-active and the best comparator. TOST ±0.5 % is secondary only. "Advantage not shown" is never written as "equal".
- **C2:** five NEW independent light seeds RL-H_t4…t8 for confirmation (the frozen Q7b recipe).
- **C3:** teleport margin explicit (mean ≤ R + 0.1); this corrects the worker's wrong "no increase" wording. The B-ETA effect is labelled joint. P-cyc is selected with the fleet.
- Self-checks S9–S14 are added. | protocol A1 |

| C-69 (19:46, 04/10) | **Corrections to C-68 and the worker's 19:33 reading** (review `…/catchup/M3_DEV_FAILURE_REVIEW_AND_NEXT_STEP_20261004.md`, checked against the DEV rows and the code).

(1) **WITHDRAWN: "the frozen R10 fleet does not convert accurate SPaT into benefit under short greens".** Counterexample in the same report: under P-cyc (exact SPaT, the controller independent of the fleet) the fleet contribution vs σ\* under the same controller is −8.16 / −8.29 / −7.65 / −6.53 / −6.36 % (G = 10…30 s). Under W-c it is −1.5 / −5.0 / −5.1 %. Green length is NOT established as the cause. A hypothesis the data suggest, NOT tested: under the unchanged RL-H light, the fleet contribution rises monotonically with the forecast quantile (F-q0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3 / 0.5: −0.47 / −0.01 / +1.85 / +5.71 %). So the DIRECTION of forecast error (green predicted later) may drive the harm.

(2) **NEW correction (worker's own error, not raised by the review): M3-3 "fleet contribution" is NOT clean for any E arm.** With σ\* cars, CarRLG has `agents` but no policy net, so the E light's receiver / active input sums are 0, while ETA sums are still counted. With the R10 fleet they are not 0. The light therefore acts on different inputs in the two runs, and (arm, fleet) − (arm, σ\*) for E arms is a JOINT system effect, like B-ETA. Only B was labelled joint. The E-arm M3-3 numbers in C-68 (e.g. E-receiver +5.06 %, E-uniform −3.76 %) are relabelled JOINT. The clean fleet-contribution rows are those whose controller does not read fleet telemetry: F-q, D, P-cyc, and W-c (closed-loop via queues only).

(3) **The worker's claim "ETA has no right to know the fleet policy" is false for the implemented arms.** Under A1 B4, every E arm (E0, uniform, ETA, active, receiver) receives the same 5 inputs, including the receiver-sensitivity and active sums. The arms differ only in the penalty weight.

(4) **Over-generalisation withdrawn.** "Every light-side mechanism costs more than it returns" is narrowed to: the recipes and grids tried (λ ∈ {1, 4}, penalty / base 1.6–11×; fixed-W 5–15; B-ETA 10–15; P-cyc 10–30) did not beat R on DEV. Separately, the plain physical fine-tune (E0-physical) degrades the light (city TL +4.1, gridlock under σ\* on 3 / 15 seeds). That is a fine-tuning-recipe failure independent of the penalty.

(5) **Decision (post-DEV, not a registered futility rule):** M3 version d1d76226 + A1 + A1b STOPS after the DEV screen. No confirmation is run, and the 510–529 panel and the five new light seeds stay unused for this version. The two-tier gate is adopted for any future M3 version:
- tier 1, "the method helps": vs R, E0 and the best comparator;
- tier 2, "receiver-awareness helps": vs uniform, ETA and active.

The pilot fails tier 1. | review + DEV rows |

| C-71 (19:56, 04/10) | **The two C-70 checks were run.**

**(1) Forecast timing convention** (`CITY_forecast_convention_check.py`). Offline, on all 52 RL-H traces, with the TRUE occurrence duration in place of the quantile, so model error is removed:
- **ttr (green end) is correct in 100 %** of 2.41 M points. The worker's suspicion of "ttr +1 s" was WRONG.
- **ttg during green is 1 s EARLY in 100 %** of 2.41 M points. A setPhase-initiated yellow is observed for 4 s, so the green onset of the other movement is at r + YELLOW + OBS **+ 1**.
- ttg during yellow is correct in 100 % of 0.64 M points.

By the same reasoning (not run separately): the policy forecast D has ttg correct and ttr 1 s late; the W-c / B commitments (Ts − t; Ts − t + 4) are correct, and S5 verified the green end.

- M2b fc50 / fc20;
- M3 F-q;
- the E arms' deployed provider, and the E tickets' promised ends (T̂ is 1 s early vs observation, inside τ = 2);
- the W-c / B pre-commitment fallback.

The results stand as evaluations of "this forecast service with this bias", NOT of a convention-correct forecast.

Commitments: receiver 73 396, eta 95 734, random 78 368. Violations: 0 in every arm.

- Receiver vs W-c5 (the best M3 comparator): +5.60 %. Receiver vs eta: +2.44 %. Receiver vs random: +4.66 %.
- **P3 FAILS:** commitment rates per decision tick are receiver 0.189 vs random 0.244. p_j was set from the measure-mode eligible rate (0.248). Under its own commitments the receiver fires less; the cause is NOT established. So the random ablation commits about 29 % more often per tick, and still costs less.
- **P-M3′-1** violations = 0 HOLDS; the fleet contribution is negative (benefit) but small. **P-M3′-2 FAILS** (light cost +6.2 > W-c5's +5.1). **P-M3′-3** (low prior) FAILS. **P-M3′-4 FAILS.**
- **Reading (hypothesis, NOT tested):**
  - A design fact from the code: h_D starts at DT_L, so every commitment holds the green at least 5 s. A commitment issued at a tick where RL-H chose switch therefore delays that switch by at least 5 s.
  - The receiver trigger fires when a fleet car approaches on RED, which is plausibly when RL-H wants to switch. That would explain why the receiver costs more than random with fewer commitments (clean +6.17 vs +4.90).
  - The sidecars do not log the overridden action, so the share of commitments issued against a switch decision is UNMEASURED. This is not claimed as the mechanism.
  - Counterexample check: random (blind) still costs +4.90 city TL, so most of the cost does not require the receiver correlation.
- **M3′ stops after DEV** (no confirmation, a post-DEV decision).

| C-84 (21:31, 04/10) | **Audit finding (demonstrated bug, one branch): the convention-corrected provider `CITY_spat_forecast_c` leaves the YELLOW → green-AFTER-next branch 1 s early.**

**The branch.** The light is in yellow after green g1, and the link is served only by g1, so its next green comes after g2 and g2's yellow. The formula is (Y − s) + OBS + full(g2) + Y. With the TRUE g2 duration in place of the quantile, formula − observed = **−1.0 s on 103 464 / 103 464** observations (RL-H:t4 TRAIN traces). The cause is the same as C-71: g2 ends by setPhase, so its yellow is observed Y + 1. `_c` adds +1 only when the light is in a green.

**Checks that missed it.** KF-0 and KA-2 test ttr, green-branch ttg and yellow → NEXT-green ttg only. Their negative control covered the green branch only. This is a check-coverage gap.

**Exposure.** The yellow share of light-seconds is shown in the run output (about 22 % on t4). Only queries during yellows, for links of the green that just ended, hit this branch.

**Affected:**
- F1 DEV (C-78) and F1-A1 (running): these are trained and evaluated with the same provider, so the comparisons are internally consistent. Their claims must read "convention-corrected in the green branch; one yellow branch remains 1 s early (C-84)".
- M3 Fq / W-c fallback / M2b: these used the uncorrected provider, so the branch was already wrong there, along with the green branch.

**Not affected:** R rows, M2a / M2 on spider, RPC and WCK (commitment-or-unavailable), D-dir (oracle).

**Action.** F1-A1 is NOT changed mid-run (a frozen registered confirmation). A fully corrected provider (`_c2`) is a candidate for any NEW registered run only. The direction of the bias (early) is the harmful one per D-dir, so the F1 numbers are, if anything, conservative for "corrected" information. This is not tested. | offline check on `f1a1_20261004/traces` |

**M3 FINAL STATUS (finish-line definition):** NOT MET, as a CONFIRMED negative with a named reason.
- Under RL-H on grid4, publishing certain timing from the learned light helps the frozen SPaT fleet (−1.2 … −2.4 % agent fuel from the messages alone).
- But holding the learned light to keep its promises costs more (+5.6 … +10.6 % agent fuel, +5.6 … +7.2 s/veh city TL).
- So the frozen fleet ends up worse off than with no SPaT at all (WC5 +3.0 %, RPC +9.3 %).
- Consistent with: all E-family arms (DEV, C-68), B-ETA (DEV), RPC tier-2 ablations (DEV, C-79), and spider 4a (C-82, where the fleet gain was ≈ 0).
- The retrained-fleet reference (more update rights) gives a replicated fuel benefit but misses the agent-TL margin (C-85).
- **There is no confirmed "winning method".**

**M4 FINAL STATUS (spider, an independent own-built network):**
- light upgrade works (C-76);
- the M2 compatibility loss and harm replicate (C-81);
- the M3 light-side negative replicates (C-82);
- the learned R9 fleet does not replicate (C-80);
- the rule's TL parity is marginal (C-81 correction);
- 4b was not run (its registered condition failed, C-85). | report_m3nc.txt |

| C-92 (23:54) | **M3′-D runner BUG (mine; demonstrated): 24 / 32 closed-loop jobs never ran.**

- **Cause:** for cells without `--record`, the job line ended with a trailing blank (empty $REC). `xargs -L 1` treats a trailing blank as a continuation, so pairs of lines merged ("unrecognized arguments: C01_k0_s13_sh"). Only the C11 cells (which have --record) ran.
- The report then failed (missing file); no verdict was produced.
- **Fix:** a new runner `m3pd_20261004/RUN_fix.sh` writes job lines without trailing blanks and runs only the missing cells (existing C11 outputs are kept). It is queued as `005_M3pD_fix.sh` in the night queue. RUN.sh is not edited.
- **Lesson:** never end an `xargs -L` job line with an optional, possibly empty field. | dev/run.log |

| C-96 (00:27, 05/10) | **H3 continuation opportunity (queue job 010; PROTOCOL_CITY_M3_H3_OPPORTUNITY_20261004 as amended; KH-1 PLACEBO == I0 bit-identical, KH-3 prefix identical: PASS)** (`m3h3_20261004/report_m3h3.txt`, `h3_results.json`). 96 situations (k 0 / 1 × seeds 0–3 × 12 situations, 48 eligible + 48 random ticks), options Z / H5 / H10 / H15 / P, full-episode reruns from t = 0.

- **Registered verdict: OPPORTUNITY YES** (oracle-best TL-constrained option beats I0 in 0.88 of eligible situations; bootstrap lower bound of the mean gain > 0).
- **This verdict does NOT establish a usable opportunity.** The data show strong chaos:
  - one intervention changes **640–900 of the 1800 vehicles**;
  - random ticks give nearly the same oracle-best share (**0.81**) as eligible ticks (0.88);
  - every option's mean full-episode effect is tiny and of the same sign in both groups (agent fuel −0.03 … −0.15 %, city TL −0.03 … −0.08 s).
- **Reading (audit `METHOD_HYPOTHESES_NIGHT_20261004` §5, plus this row):**
  - (1) The hindsight max over 5 noisy branches is a privileged benchmark, not a lower bound for a deployable rule.
  - (2) The "affected vehicles" cohort depends on the treatment, so its per-vehicle margins are not the deployment estimand.
  - (3) The 96 situations are nested in 8 (k, seed) episodes, and I0 is ONE chaotic draw per episode shared by its 12 situations. A same-sign shift of all options can be I0 luck.
- **Consequence:** the decisive test is out of sample (N5 / N6). Rules are fitted on seeds 0–1 and realized on seeds 2–3. They are compared with the situation's mean over options (which removes the shared I0 draw) and with random choice, not with the oracle. | report_m3h3.txt; h3_results.json |
| C-97 (00:27, 05/10) | **N1 tail-support abstention: REJECTED as a material harm source** (queue job 020; PROTOCOL_CITY_N1_TAIL_SUPPORT_20261005; DEV 600–611 generated after a non-existence check; KN1-1 all == c2 bit-identical PASS; 16 / 16 grid4 files COMPLETE, 12 / 12 seeds, 0 unfinished, 0 teleports; spider 4 files COMPLETE) (`n1_20261005/report_n1.txt`).

|---|---|---|
| Fleet queries answered with no tail support (pool + none) | **0.11 %** | 0.21 % |
| c2-all vs R, agent fuel | +0.43 % [−0.09, +1.04]; agent TL **+1.18** [+0.93, +1.39]; human TL −1.12 | +14.37 %; agent TL +29.3 |
| tail − all (paired), agent fuel | **+0.01 %** [−0.03, +0.07] | +0.01 % [−0.35, +0.43] |
| strict − all (paired) | −0.03 % [−0.21, +0.15] | −0.06 % |

- **Decision rule** (SUPPORTED iff tail exposure ≥ 1 % and tail − all fuel upper < 0): exposure 0.11 % → REJECTED.
- **Diagnostic of where the error is (offline, k0):** local-support queries (67 % of queries) have bias −11.2 s, MAE 11.8 s, within ±1 s only 31 %. Program-derived queries (33 %) have MAE 5.1 s. On spider the local MAE is 31 s.
  - The forecast error is in the supported (local) region, not the tail.
  - This points to N2 (state / target), not to support.
- **Scope:** the tail fallback is replaced; the c2 table itself is unchanged; q = 0.1 only. | report_n1.txt; g4/, sp/ |
| C-98 (00:30, 05/10) | **N5 / N6 out of sample on the H3 branches: OOS OPPORTUNITY NO; N5 REJECTED; N6 REJECTED** (PROTOCOL_CITY_N5N6_OOS_20261005, registered before the per-seed split; `scripts/CITY_n5n6_oos.py`; `m3h3_20261004/report_n5n6.txt`). Checks KN5-1 / KN5-2 / KN6-0 (P == Z when RL-H would switch) PASS. Two-fold cross-fitting by seed; cluster bootstrap over the 8 (k, seed) episodes.

| Rule (out of sample, all 96) | Gain vs I0 (agent fuel %, + = saving) | vs RANDOM option |
|---|---|---|
| RANDOM option | +0.075 [−0.043, +0.194] | — |
| FIXED (training-best option) | +0.091 | +0.016 [−0.028, +0.044] |
| SENS (near-only) | +0.071 | −0.004 |
| COST (\|Q-gap\|-only) | +0.053 | −0.022 |
| RC (receiver + cost cells) | +0.113 | +0.038 [−0.013, +0.097] |
| LEARN (ridge per option) | +0.073 | −0.002 |
| STRAT (by RL-H action) | +0.102 | +0.027 [−0.035, +0.085] |

- **The decisive fact is a noise floor.** The within-situation SD of option outcomes is 0.235 % (mean), against 0.040 % between option means.
  - The best fixed option (H10, +0.116 %) does not beat a within-situation permutation of option labels (null 95th percentile +0.136 %, p = 0.29).
  - A single 5–15 s commitment changes the episode by ~0.1 %, while the chaotic divergence it triggers is ~0.24 %. Per-decision counterfactual labels therefore have SNR ≈ 0.2 here.
- **N6 description:** in the would-switch stratum, holds did not cost more than switch-now (H15 +0.16, Z +0.05); in the would-keep stratum, a forced switch Z ≈ 0. Everything is inside the noise.
- **Reading:**
  - The H3 "OPPORTUNITY YES" (C-96) was max-of-noise.
  - Commitment selection at the single-decision level is not learnable from branch labels in this simulator. Learning it would need ~20 or more replicate noise draws per situation.
  - This is a structural reason for the method design: candidates must be scored at the POLICY level (paired full episodes, as N4 / N8 do), not by per-decision critics. It mirrors the September selector failure (within-state ranking).
- **Scope:** the H3 option menu, 8 episodes, one fleet per light; N7 (accumulation) is separate. | report_n5n6.txt; n5n6.json |
| C-99 (00:33, 05/10) | **N2 state-conditioned direct-TTG forecast: N2 SUPPORTED (registered rule: state − c2 at the same q); NOT an M3 candidate (agent TL); FORECAST BETTER = NO** (queue job 030; PROTOCOL_CITY_N2_STATE_FORECAST_20261005; KN2-1 / KN2-2 provider == offline features and predictions: 0 mismatches on 9 761 / 9 671 rows; 285 censored rows excluded offline) (`n2_20261005/report_n2.txt`).

- **Model:** torch MLP quantile net 64-64, monotone q0.1 / q0.5.
  - Features: phase one-hot, age, kind, halting / counts of the current and target greens, recent green-length history, light one-hot.
  - Trained on TRAIN seeds 0–5, validated on 6–7 (episodes split), on 16 collection runs with the fleet using c2.
  - sklearn was unavailable; nothing was installed.
- **Offline, query-weighted MAE (fleet queries):**
  - q0.5: state 4.57 / 4.32 s vs age-only 6.76 / 6.42 s (−32 % / −33 %);
  - q0.1: state 7.03 / 6.49 vs 6.65 / 6.21 (+6 % / +5 %, worse; MAE is not the right loss for a 0.1 quantile).
  - So the registered "≥ 10 % at both q" fails.

|---|---|---|---|---|

| C-100 (00:44, 05/10) | **N2M mechanism of N2's agent-time cost (queue job 031; PROTOCOL_CITY_N2M_MECHANISM_20261005; KM-1 recording == R and KM-2 σ\* replay == R: PASS; 0 teleports, 0 unfinished)** (`n2m_20261005/report_n2m.txt`).

**M-HD hidden demand: SUPPORTED (q:1:0).**
- The light-input adapter q:1:0 counts vehicles at 0.1–2 m/s as halting, in front of the frozen RL-H. It changes N2's agent TL by **−0.80** [−1.16, −0.51] at **+0.84 %** fuel (vs N2 alone).
- The adapter alone (σ\* fleet) changes almost nothing: fuel +0.27 %, TLs within ±0.25.
- **N2 + q:1:0 vs R:** fuel **−2.55 %** (up −1.72); city TL +0.30 (up +0.43); agent TL **+1.51** (up +1.67); human −0.95. **NOT a candidate** (agent TL).
- q:1:1 overshoots: fuel −0.80 %, agent TL +1.00.

**M-FB: FLEET-INTRINSIC.** With R's light schedule replayed (open loop):
- N2 gives agent fuel −0.12 % (no gain), agent TL **+2.72** [+1.88, +3.50], human TL +0.82.
- c2 q0.1 gives fuel +1.08 % and agent TL −0.07.
- Closed loop − open loop for N2: fuel **−3.35**, human TL −2.11, agent TL −0.41. So the LIGHT'S REACTION to the eco fleet is what produces N2's fuel gain (and the humans' gain).
- The agent-time cost is present without any light reaction. With c2 (early-biased q0.1) the open-loop time cost is absent. This points at the fleet's response to N2's (median, later) forecasts, or at the fleet's own trade-off.

**Next (registered as Amendment X3):** exact future on the same replay (C-61 oracle). It decides between forecast error and the frozen fleet's time-for-fuel trade-off under RL-H's short greens (mean green 15 s vs 42 s under FT). | report_n2m.txt |

|---|---|

- **Reading:**
  - Single-decision branch effects are noise-level (C-98: SNR ≈ 0.2). Their sum says nothing about the policy-level effect, which is strongly harmful.
  - A second, smaller instance: two service components are not additive. N2 alone −3.36 % / agent TL +2.31; the light adapter alone +0.27 % / −0.04; together −2.55 % / +1.51, i.e. interaction +0.54 % fuel and −0.76 s TL (C-100).
- **Consequence for any candidate:** it must be evaluated (and tuned) as a whole policy over full episodes. This holds for N4 and N8, which are already designed that way. | m3pd dev sidecars; h3_results.json |
| C-102 (00:49, 05/10) | **X2 forecast-error breakdown (offline; audit M3_DEV_FAILURE §2 asked for it; `scripts/CITY_n2_breakdown.py`, `n2_20261005/x2_breakdown.json`).** N2 validation episodes (TRAIN 6–7), query-weighted, by event × light state × realized horizon.

- **Age-only c2 is EXACT during yellow for horizons < 15 s (MAE 0.00); the N2 state model is not** (MAE 0.7–1.0 s at 0–5 s and 1.7–5.4 s at 5–15 s). These cells carry about 20 % of the fleet's query weight.
  - The state model ignores the deterministic structure (fixed 3 s yellows; known next-green onset).
  - **Engineering fix, registered for a later arm:** N2-hybrid answers deterministic yellow queries from the program and uses N2 elsewhere. This is not a novelty claim.
- **Where N2 helps (q0.5):**
  - ttr at the end of a green (0–5 s): 2.0 vs 7.4–8.1 s;
  - ttg 5–15 s during the other green: 2.3–2.5 vs 5.9–6.5 s;
  - long horizons > 30 s: 17–23 vs 26–33 s.
- **Both models are strongly early-biased at long horizons** (bias −17 … −40 s for > 30 s): the heavy tail of RL-H's green / red durations.

  - N2's agent-time cost (C-99 / C-100) comes from forecast ERRORS.
  - The binding lever is forecast accuracy at the queries the fleet acts on (X2: long horizons are early-biased by 17–40 s; yellow cells were fixed by HYB).
  - This motivates a policy-aware forecaster: the infrastructure owns RL-H, so its Q-gap at the query second is a causal feature (HONEST-CAV-like; prior art, a component, not a novelty claim). | report_x3.txt |
| C-108 (01:03, 05/10) | **TSI (two-sided interface) registered as the M3 candidate; selection → variant → confirmation queued** (PROTOCOL_CITY_TSI_20261005 + Amendment A1, written before any TSI run).

  - Fleet side: the hybrid forecast (exact where the schedule is determined, the state model elsewhere).
  - Light side: the demand adapter q:A:B.

  - (A, B) from an 8-point grid, by a rule aligned with the gate: agent / city / human TL points ≤ +0.20, then the most fuel;
  - then one-shot vs refit-under-TSI forecaster, by the same rule.

  - frozen fleets R10 s213 / s313 / s413 / s513 (never used in M3 / N development);
  - traffic 700–719 (generated after a non-existence check);
  - crossed 5 × 4 × 20 per arm; arms R / TSI / HYB / ADP;
  - per-light forecaster fitted on TRAIN only.
- **Primary:** TSI vs R meets every M3 limit at one-sided 95 %.
- **Queue order:** 034 N3 (running) → 032c TSI-SELECT → 032d TSI-REFIT → 032e TSI-CONFIRM → 035 N8. | protocol; queue |

| C-113 (01:23, 05/10) | **Corrections from the user's review `…/catchup/TSI_N3_REVIEW_20261005.md`, plus one error of mine found while checking.** No implementation is changed during the TSI confirmation.

**(1) "Exact in yellow" overclaimed (C-102, C-106, N4 A2, TSI).**
- The hybrid sends EVERY yellow-phase query to c2. c2 is exact only when the target green is the phase right after the yellow ("direct").
- **Offline check (k0 validation, q0.5):**

| Yellow-phase query | Query weight | c2 MAE |
|---|---|---|
| direct (ttg < 5 s) | 61 115 | **0.0** |
| through an intermediate green, 5–15 s | 31 799 | **3.69 s** |
| through an intermediate green, ≥ 15 s | 29 304 | **14.4 s** |

- **My X2 row (C-102) was wrong:** its "MAE 0.00 at 5–15 s during yellow" came from the q0.1 table. TSI uses q0.5, where c2 has 3.72 s in that cell and the state model is better (2.13 s).
- **Correct description:** "exact where the schedule to the event is determined (the next green after a yellow). For a link that must wait through an intermediate green, c2's age-only forecast is used." The hybrid helps through the direct cell; in the 5–15 s cell it is worse than N2.
- Not changed mid-confirmation. A direct-only hybrid is a possible later variant, needing a new registration and new data.

**(2) KT-1 not executed.** The TSI protocol lists KT-1 (offline / runtime parity), but runner 032f has only the smoke and KT-2.
- **Added as queue job 033_TSI_KT1**, which runs right after the confirmation panel:
  - KN2-style parity of the per-light forecaster on TRAIN seed 0 for lights t4 and t8;
  - gate `all` == N2 rows on the same runs.
- No self-check claim before it passes.

**(3) N3 wording (C-109).**
- "Red waits > 30 s up ~70×" counts SCORED QUERIES (every 5th query), not vehicles or independent waits. Total queries also rise.
- The shares are **3.34 % (published) vs 0.089 % (shadow)** of scored ttg queries. Still a very large shift.
- "Calibrating cannot fix them" → "this one-round refit recipe did not help". It does not show that no forecast refit can.

**(4) The TSI margin (C-110).**
- +0.20 is the SELECT point rule; +0.50 is the one-sided confirmation upper bound.
- The point gap to the confirmation threshold is 0.32 s/veh, with the CI and the transfer to new seeds unknown. "Thin" referred to that, not to 0.02 s.
- (2, 0) is kept; no switch to B > 0.

**(5) No cross-panel claims.**
- C-106's "HYBQ saves more fuel than F1-B" compared different panels (600–611 vs 550–564, different lights / fleets): withdrawn.
- TSI vs F1-B needs the same panel.

**(6) If TSI confirms**, still needed for a method paper:
- the paired component reading (TSI − HYB, TSI − ADP);
- a strong tuned adapter comparator with the same information and budget;
- another setting;
- the costs of the forecaster fit, the sensors and inference. | review file; offline check above |

**Per light (TSI-2 − R), agent fuel / agent TL:** t4 −4.54 / −0.22; t5 −3.35 / −0.32; t6 −3.79 / −0.24; t7 −4.09 / +0.18; t8 −3.45 / −0.47.

**What this establishes (scope: grid4, learned light RL-H, frozen R10 PPO fleets, this panel):**
- A two-sided interface, with neither learned population retrained, recovers a large share of the frozen fleet's fuel benefit after the learned light upgrade. It improves city and human time, with agent time non-inferior.
- Both components are necessary:
  - without the fleet-side service there is no fuel gain;
  - without the light-side adapter agents lose ~2 s.
- On the same panel it matches the fleet retrained under the new light on fuel (CI includes 0) and beats it on every time endpoint.

**Caveats (keep):**
- Second attempt, after the TSI FAIL C-114. The family-wise bound across the two attempts is 0.075.
- One network.
- The confirmation lights and fleets were reused from the first attempt; the traffic is fresh.
- The adapter's speed bins assume per-lane speed sensing at the light. | report_tsi2_confirm.txt |

| HYBDNL (both) | −4.01 % | +0.02 |

- **C-113's "direct-only" refinement:** no endpoint effect at this scale.
- **The left-turn ('g' must-yield) hypothesis:** removing SPaT on permissive lefts costs fuel and does not cut agent time. It is not a material source of the agent-time cost.
- **The source is the hidden demand / green lengthening** (C-117), which the per-light adapter (TSI-2) addressed. | report_tsiv.txt |

**Reading:**
- The two-sided interface helps all three frozen receiver classes the repo has (PPO residual, 4-parameter search policy, rule). The gain is smaller for receivers that use SPaT less aggressively.
- It holds at 25–75 % penetration (the forecaster was fitted at 50 %).
- The state forecaster is the necessary fleet-side component: age-only + adapter harms.
- H-PATH is closed: a fleet adapted to the new light does no harm under the old light, so the fleet-first order is safe in this setting. | report_gen.txt |
| C-122 (04:56, 05/10) | **PERF: the light-side adapter REDUCES the publication sensitivity of the forecast target (SUPPORTED)** (PROTOCOL_CITY_PERF_20261005, registered after the research pass, before the run; DEV 600–611; 0 FAIL) (`perf_20261005/report_perf.txt`).

| pub − shadow (forecast published vs computed only) | with adapter q:2:0 | without (N3) | difference |
|---|---|---|---|
| ttg MAE at the fleet's queries | +0.73 s | +1.32 s | **−0.59** [−0.88, −0.35] |
| share of scored waits > 30 s | +0.64 pts | +3.25 pts | −2.60 pts |
| share of scored waits 15–30 s | +4.38 pts | +6.40 pts | −2.02 pts |

- **Reading (mechanism for the two-sided design):**
  - Publishing the forecast shifts its own target (performative feedback). The light-side adapter halves that shift, so the fleet-side forecast stays closer to valid once published.
  - This matches the small-gain / performative-stability framing: lowering the light's gain to the fleet's eco-approach.
  - Mechanism evidence on DEV, not a confirmation. | report_perf.txt |

**Conclusion for the paper (scope):**
- On spider (multi-green learned lights RLK, rule fleet), the hidden-demand hazard is far larger: the service alone costs ~58 s/veh. The two-parameter adapter removes ~97 % of it, but a residual agent-time cost of ≈ +1.3 s/veh remains.
- At B = 0.5 fuel still falls 1.7 %. A stronger adapter costs time by itself.
- So the TSI METHOD is confirmed on grid4 only. Its MECHANISM (both halves necessary; the service alone harmful) replicates on spider; its limits do not.
- The adapter class (speed bins in front of current / next-green halting) is likely too coarse for RLK's multi-green input; untested hypothesis. | report_sptsi2.txt |

p7 spider diagnosis is drawn when W8-1 lands.

**How it is built:**
- Every number is read from the registered reports through the dashboard parser.

| C-138 (17:0x, 05/10) | **W8-1 SPDIAG: spider fails through closed-loop forecastability, not headroom, and not because of the 3-green signals** (job 070, 16:52–16:53; full rows: seeds 1100–1103, not smoke, 1800 vehicles per run; `w8_20261005/spdiag/report_spdiag.txt`).
- **D1:** the service lengthens spider greens much more than grid4's: HYBK 28.7 vs R 18.9 s, +52 %; grid4 HYB 17.1 vs 15.0, +14 %. TSIK cuts halting from 8.25 to 3.90.
- **D2:** at the fleet's queries the published N2K forecast errs by MAE **21.5 s**, bias −15.5 s (predicted earlier than realized), 69 % of errors > 5 s; grid4 is 3.02 s. K = 2: MAE 23.6; K = 3: 19.5.
- **D3, oracle on the replayed schedule:** exact timing is worth **−7.39 %** (RLK11) and **−6.63 %** (RLK111) agent fuel, like grid4's −5.6 … −6.6 %. RLK111's oracle also costs agent TL +1.34.
  - **KH-1 FAILED on both lights:** the open-loop replay does not reproduce the recorded σ* run bit for bit. D3 is internally paired (oracle and σ* on the same replay), but the replayed schedule is an approximation of the closed loop. Disclosed.
- **D4:** the harm is in the 2-green signals: K2ONLY agent TL +1.43 / +0.94 vs K3ONLY +0.16 / −0.37. The 3-green-only service saves fuel (−1.80 / −2.43 %) within the time limits on TUNE.
- **Registered reading:** not a headroom failure (D3 shows a benefit). It is a **forecastability failure** (D2 errors about 7 × grid4's), and **not** attributable to the K = 3 signals (D4: the harm is mostly in the 2-green arm).
- The D1 / D2 pattern (greens lengthened under the service, forecasts early by 15 s) is the closed-loop account of §diag, at a larger amplitude: the service is self-defeating on spider. | report_spdiag.txt |

| C-143 (20:1x, 05/10) | **Corrections: DCA2 tuning was invalid; the W8-1 D2 forecast-error comparison is not valid.** Both were found by an independent audit, a parallel session that edited `scripts/CITY_fig_paper.py` at 19:52 and wrote `CITY_dca3_run.py` / `CITY_w7_dca3_tune.py`. I verified both against the code and the rows.

**(1) DCA2 (C-134): a demonstrated bug in my tuner.**
- `CITY_w7_dca2_tune.py` ran the R reference rollouts through `CITY_eval_f1.py`, which ignores `DCA2_END`. The references therefore ran the full 5400 s: `roll_t4/R_s660`, n = 1800, 0 unfinished.
- The candidates were truncated at 1350 s: `r0_A0_base`, n = 675, 50 unfinished.
- Every tuning return thus compared a quarter-horizon candidate with a full-horizon reference.
- Also: the transforms were initialised to all-zero parameters (`torch.zeros`), not the author's LayerNorm initialisation, and the author's nan_to_num / [−10, 10] bound was missing.
- **Consequence:** "a faithful DC-Ada port missed the limit" is **withdrawn**. The DCA2 confirmation rows (760–779) remain a correct measurement of the transforms that this buggy tuning produced, labelled audit-invalid. No valid published-method comparison exists. The corrected code (DCA3) has not been run by me.

**(2) W8-1 D2 (C-138): a protocol-vs-implementation mismatch.**
- The rows D2 used (`provider_rows_s*.json`, written by `CITY_n2k.make_provider`) are the forecaster's raw predictions for **every event head** at queried light-seconds, not the answers served to the fleet. The direct-hybrid branches are excluded, and the buffer is capped at the **first 20 000 rows per run**.
- The protocol asked for the provider's answers at the fleet's queries.
- **Consequence:** MAE 21.5 s is not comparable with grid4's 3.02 s (served answers over full episodes). The "forecastability failure" reading, which rested on D2, is **withdrawn**.
- What stands, descriptive: D1 (greens +52 % under the service); D3 (exact-timing headroom −7.39 / −6.63 %, on an approximate replay, KH-1 FAIL); D4 (the agents' time cost arises with the service on the 2-green signals, not the 3-green ones).
- The cause of the spider failure is therefore **not established**.

Paper, figures and claim statements corrected accordingly. The figure script already labels DCA2 "audit-invalid" and omits p7 (the audit's edit), and that edit is kept. | code + rows |

|---|---|

**Synthesis:** the two other networks fail for different reasons.
- **spider:** a publication-induced forecast shift (+7.8 s; greens +50 %) and humans held behind commanded agents on one-lane edges (5 × grid4; human TL +32 under the service).
- **rand3:** the learned light's long, variable greens are hard to forecast from these features even without publication (shadow 4 × grid4), and the calibration grid had no qualified point on 4 / 5 lights.

Lane count alone is not the common cause: rand3 is single-lane but holds few humans at its low demand. | report_diag.txt |

**Audit:**
- Lights RLK2L 11 / 111 at 120 / 120 episodes.
- Demand generated fresh at 20:41 after the non-existence check (37 files = 8 TRAIN + 5 DEV + 4 TUNE + 20 confirmation).
- KS-1 PASS (0 feature and 0 prediction mismatches over 6 846 rows); KS-2 provider counters valid, 0 exceptions.

**Preconditions:**
- P1: the learned light beats FT by −39.8 s/veh.
- P2: the rule fleet saves fuel vs σ* under FT (−4.48 % [−4.72, −4.24]).
- FT city TL 61.1 vs 93.7 on the 1-lane build: lower congestion, as expected.

**Calibration:** on both lights no grid point qualified on TUNE. The rule's fallback (minimum agent TL) chose **(1, 0.5)**, which happens to be the W9-5 global setting.

**Confirmation** (1110–1129; lights 11 / 111 × the rule fleet):

| Arm − R | agent fuel % | city TL (up) | agent TL (up) | human TL (up) | all limits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **TSIK** | −1.49 [−1.75, −1.19] | +0.11 | +0.16 (**+0.42**) | −0.07 | **PASS** |
| HYBK | −2.06 | | **+4.24** | | fails |
| ADPK | **+0.25** | | | | fails (no fuel) |

**Greens:** R 16.6, HYBK 18.7 (+13 %, as grid4 +14 %), TSIK 15.8. On the 1-lane build: 18.9 → 28.7 (+52 %).

**Reading (registered):** the lane count is a sufficient cause of the spider failure under this pipeline. The single factor includes overtaking and capacity, plus the disclosed inner-ring phase change (B1–B6 have 2 greens instead of 3).

**Caveats:**
- Agent TL upper +0.42 is close to the margin.
- The fuel saving is smaller than on grid4.
- Two lights × one rule fleet.
- No grid point qualified on TUNE.

Combined with W10-2 / 3 (spider: performative shift plus single-lane holding) and W10-2b (rand3: inherent forecastability), the transfer condition is a road on which the fleet's slower approach can be overtaken or absorbed, **and** a deployed signal that is forecastable without publication. | report_sp2l.txt |

**Reading:**
- On rand3 the binding constraint was the light-side weight. Counting vehicles at 2–5 m/s with weight 1 (B ≥ 1, outside the registered grid) was needed, not a better forecast.

**Updated synthesis:**

| Network | Lanes | Demand | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| grid4 | 2 | | passes |
| spider-2L | 2 | | passes |
| rand3 | 1 | low | passes with a wider adapter grid |
| spider | 1 | higher | fails even with the forecast shift removed (W10-5): humans held behind slow agents |

The remaining failure condition is **one-lane roads with enough following traffic to be held**. The per-network adapter range must be wide enough (B up to 1–2). | report_n3b.txt |

The green side therefore always looks loaded; the frozen DQN holds green and the red side starves. Consistent with this, on spider the shortest τ is worst (τ 3: agent TL +23 to +27) and the longest is least bad. This is a design weakness of the specified adapter, not an implementation bug: the code implements the registered formula, and KPQ-1 passed. The optional green cap would not fix it. The fix would be to count approaching vehicles only on the red or yellow approach (or on a green approach only behind a standing queue), which needs a new amendment. | `w15_20261007/pq/{grid4,spider}/conf/report_pq.txt` |
